# Per-file obligation audit — `src/provider/cipher.rs`

> **Status: this is a file-level obligation audit, not `SAFETY_DISCHARGED`.** It enumerates and attributes the file's production unsafe sites; it does not establish whole-file safety, it does not discharge the residual obligations listed below, and it does not constitute an `INDEPENDENTLY_CHECKED` result. A file graduates only through the AGENTS.md §7 dependency-closure and independent-evidence gates.

- Census totals for this file: 1,126 operations; 1,098 production; 28 test-only.
- Production by kind: `FC 193`, `HR 11`, `UB 422`, `UF 443`, `UI 28`, `UT 1` (total 1,098).
- Test module begins at line 22038; all test-only sites are at or after that line and are excluded.
- Grouping: one row per `(kind, enclosing function)`; 930 groups cover all 1,098 production sites. This is not 1,098 individual rows.
- **No exported symbols.** The file contains no `#[no_mangle]` and no `#[export_name]`; all 309 `extern "C"` definitions are `pub(crate)`/private dispatch callbacks. No row therefore claims an external-C-ABI establishment. `ESTABLISHED` rows rest only on in-file code/ownership facts.
- **Census attribution artifact.** The census's enclosing-function heuristic attributes sites inside `macro_rules!` bodies and `mod legacy` impls to the nearest preceding named function. The groups named `ossl_prov_cipher_hw_aria_ctr` (8 `UI`, 2 `UB`, 2 `FC`) and `ossl_prov_cipher_hw_aes_siv` (2 `UB`, 1 `FC`) are legacy `CipherDestroy` impls and macro bodies, not those selectors. Several real groups also mix their own body with macro-body sites (`ossl_tdes_set_ctx_params`, `aes_wrap_set_ctx_params`, `cts_settable_ctx_params`, `aes_xts_stream_final`, `aes_ocb_cipher`, `aes_ccm_freectx`, `aes_cbc_hmac_sha256_freectx`, `ossl_aes_gcm_siv_settable_ctx_params`, `rc2_dinit`); the affected lines are visible in the table and do not change the conservative state. Fix the census attribution before relying on the artifact rows.

The six raw-pointer flag-helper groups are `bits`, `bits_set`, `gsiv_flag`, `gsiv_set`, `cpp_flag` and `cpp_set`; each carries a `UB` site despite a safe signature. The other two `safe fn` groups are `Drop::drop` and `raise_prov` (both established by an owner/type invariant). The four `unsafe extern "C" — not exported (macro body)` groups are the census attribution artifacts under `ossl_prov_cipher_hw_aria_ctr` and `ossl_prov_cipher_hw_aes_siv`; their real enclosing items are the legacy and `siv_row!` macro bodies. All remaining groups are `unsafe fn`/`unsafe extern "C"` dispatch callbacks or `n/a` impl/trait groups.

## 4. Residual obligation register

These obligations remain after the current contracts. They are not repaired by a `SAFETY:` comment.

1. **R1 — raw owning members and self-asserted destructors.** The contexts contain raw owning members: `ProvCipherCtx::tlsmac` (`*mut c_uchar` plus `alloced`/`tlsmacsize`), `ProvAesGcmSivCtx::aad` and `ecb_ctx`, `ProvAesSivCtx::ctr`/`cbc` (EVP cipher references), the OCB L-table, and the inline key schedules. `destroy_body` frees them itself (`src/provider/cipher.rs:596-815`), and the 27 `CipherDestroy` impls are implementer assertions called from a safe `Drop` (`:520-533`). No owner slot or typed borrow enforces the relation.
2. **R2 — provider dispatch callbacks and reentrancy.** The dispatch tables install `init`, `cipher`, `copyctx`, `cleanup`, `clean_ctx`, `dupctx`/`dup_ctx`, `setkey`, `setiv`, `setaad`, and the CCM/GCM-SIV function pointers. The code commonly re-reads `ctx` fields after the callback returns; a provider callback that frees or mutates the context is not guarded. The enclosing-function `ERROR` paths and `CRYPTO_*_free` calls are also reentrant with the allocator hook (`CRYPTO_set_mem_functions`).
3. **R3 — caller-asserted lengths and pointer extents.** `keylen`/`ivlen` (`:1086`, `:3725`, `:5171`), `inl`/`outsize` (`:1209`, `:1440`, `:3914`), `aad_len`/`taglen` (`:8431`, `:9188`, `:10322`), `tlsmacsize` (`:1061`), and every `OSSL_PARAM` array (`:1473`, `:1554`) are asserted by the dispatch caller. Some helpers check one bound (`:1447`, `:3834`, `:3931`); most do not.
4. **R4 — copy/dup aliasing and partial initialisation.** `cipher_hw_*_copyctx` does `ptr::copy_nonoverlapping` over the whole context (`:2709`, `:2757`, `:2819`, `:5622`, `:5019`); a self-copy (`dst == src`) is UB and is not checked. `aes_ocb_dupctx` (`:6634`), `siv_dupctx` (`:9947`), and `aes_wrap_dupctx` (`:3684`) shallow-copy raw owning members and then repair them; failure paths release through `OwnedRaw`'s plain `CRYPTO_free` (`:578-582`), not the full destructor. `aes_siv_dupctx` takes `EVP_CIPHER_up_ref` before `ossl_siv128_copy_ctx` and leaks those references on the failure arm (`:10297-10311`).
5. **R5 — key material lifetime and cleansing.** Key schedules and GCM-SIV derived keys live inline in the context (`:2543-2596`, `:13190-13220`); the successful release is `CRYPTO_clear_free` over the whole block (`:526-531`), but a failed duplicate uses plain `CRYPTO_free` (`:581`), leaving copied key material in freed storage. `aesni_cbc_hmac_sha1_set_mac_key` replaces the authority's `OPENSSL_cleanse` with `ptr::write_bytes` (`:11225`), which the optimiser may remove as a dead store; `rc4_hmac_md5` uses `OPENSSL_cleanse` (`:21664`).
6. **R6 — safe functions hiding raw-pointer preconditions.** `bits` (`:383`) and `bits_set` (`:389`) are safe `fn`s that dereference a caller pointer; `gsiv_flag`/`gsiv_set` (`:13236`, `:13242`) and `cpp_flag`/`cpp_set` (`:15738`, `:15747`) do the same. `ctx_enc` (`:11107`) inherits the obligation through `bits`. Because the functions are safe, no caller is required to establish the precondition; this is a safe-interface soundness defect.
7. **R7 — global state and the `Sync` assertion.** `EXPORTED_CIPHERS` is a `static SyncCell<[OsslAlgorithm; 140]>` (`:14862`) whose sole-write-before-read invariant is `ossl_default_provider_init`'s contract, not a property of the type (`:14872-14877`). `unsafe impl<T> Sync for SyncCell<T>` (`:14877`) is a comment-only assertion; `exported_ciphers()` (`:14899`) hands out a raw pointer to the cell from a safe fn.
8. **R8 — constant-time tag/MAC comparison boundary.** `CRYPTO_memcmp` is used for GCM-SIV and ChaCha20-Poly1305 tag checks (`:9255`, `:13624`, `:16227`, `:16434`, `:21581`), but the surrounding `from_raw_parts` slices are built from caller/provider lengths (`:13459`, `:13550`, `:13555`). The comparison is full-length only if those lengths are the tag lengths the contract claims.
9. **R9 — output-extent under-check in the key-wrap row.** `aes_wrap_cipher` checks `outsize < inl` (`:3931`) and then calls `aes_wrap_cipher_internal` (`:3940`), whose `CRYPTO_128_wrap`/`wrap_pad` path can write `up16(inl) + 8` bytes. The internal helper has no `outsize` parameter (`:3819`) and its contract says only "`out`/`input` as the caller's contract". A caller passing `outsize == inl` can be overrun; the authority has the same shape, so this is a recorded compatibility residual, not a transcription slip.
10. **R10 — ownership adoption and transfer provenance.** `OwnedCtx::from_raw` (`:511`) and `OwnedRaw::adopt` (`:560`) are the two raw-adoption seams; every `freectx`/`dupctx` caller asserts provenance, alignment, extent and single ownership. `into_raw` (`:501`, `:570`) gives the block back to the C caller with no type-level token. The `CipherDestroy` trait is `unsafe`, but its implementers are the only place the zero-validity half is stated.

## 5. Operations not classified

All 930 groups are assigned a row. No production site in `src/provider/cipher.rs` is left unclassified. The `UNATTRIBUTED` groups are the two top-level `unsafe impl`/`unsafe trait` groups, the five artifact groups (`ossl_prov_cipher_hw_aria_ctr` `UB`/`FC`/`UI` and `ossl_prov_cipher_hw_aes_siv` `UB`/`FC`), the six safe raw-pointer flag helpers, and the four key-wrap output-extent groups (`aes_wrap_cipher`/`aes_wrap_cipher_internal` `UF`/`UB`). The remaining groups are `CONTRACTED` or `ESTABLISHED`; `CONTRACTED` is not a safety result.

## 6. Highest-risk groups

The first three are `UNATTRIBUTED`. Ranks 4 and 5 are the highest-risk `CONTRACTED` mechanisms, included because the brief asks for five risks; they are labelled explicitly.

| Rank | Group | Group IDs | File:line | Why |
|---:|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | top-level `unsafe impl`s + legacy `CipherDestroy` impls | `C008`; `C930` | src/provider/cipher.rs:14862; :14877; :21952-22029 | `UNATTRIBUTED`: `SyncCell`'s one-write-before-read invariant is a comment, `exported_ciphers()` returns a raw pointer from a safe fn, and 27 destructor bodies are implementer assertions called by a safe `Drop`. |
| 2 | safe raw-pointer flag helpers | `C001`; `C002`; `C583`; `C584`; `C676`; `C677` | src/provider/cipher.rs:383; :389; :13236; :13242; :15738; :15747 | `UNATTRIBUTED`: `bits`/`bits_set` and the GCM-SIV/ChaCha20-Poly1305 flag helpers are safe `fn`s that dereference an unvalidated caller pointer; `ctx_enc` (`:11107`) inherits the same precondition. |
| 3 | key-wrap output extent | `C183`; `C184`; `C187`; `C188` | src/provider/cipher.rs:3819; :3914; :3931; :3940 | `UNATTRIBUTED`: `outsize < inl` is checked, but wrap/wrap-pad can write `up16(inl) + 8`; the internal helper has no `outsize` parameter and states no output-extent contract. |
| 4 | `aesni_cbc_hmac_sha1_set_mac_key` | `C516`; `C517` | src/provider/cipher.rs:11185; :11225 | `CONTRACTED` (highest-risk non-`UNATTRIBUTED`): the authority's `OPENSSL_cleanse` is a `ptr::write_bytes` dead store; the HMAC key may remain in stack storage. `mac`/`len` are caller-asserted. |
| 5 | `cipher_hw_*_copyctx` and `aes_siv_dupctx` | `C122`; `C123`; `C126`; `C132`; `C463`; `C464` | src/provider/cipher.rs:2706; :2751; :2813; :10291 | `CONTRACTED` (highest-risk non-`UNATTRIBUTED`): whole-context `copy_nonoverlapping` trusts `dst != src` and initialises a `CRYPTO_malloc` block; the SIV duplicate takes `EVP_CIPHER_up_ref` before copy and leaks on failure. |

## 7. What would make this audit `INDEPENDENTLY_CHECKED`

For each group, an independent reviewer would need: the exact source and build identity; a contract witness for every precondition (a caller, a dispatch table row, an owner invariant, or an exported ABI declaration); a runtime/static check that fires on a deliberate violation (Miri/tree-borrows, ASan/guard pages, allocator witness, callback-reentrancy harness, refcount/double-free negative control); and a written disposition of every residual. For this file the minimum additional controls are: a NULL/dangling-pointer control for `bits`/`bits_set` and the flag helpers; a `dst == src` self-copy control for every `copyctx`; a failed-`CRYPTO_memdup`/failed-`dupctx` aliasing control for every duplicate; an `outsize == inl` wrap-pad guard-page control; a stack-residue/cleanse check for `set_mac_key`; and a concurrent-reader/writer control for `SyncCell`/`EXPORTED_CIPHERS`. Absent those, every group remains `CONTRACTED` or `ESTABLISHED`, and the file remains not `SAFETY_DISCHARGED`.

## 8. Source-investigation delta (2026-10-18, `integrate/safe-c33`, base `b51f84977`)

This section records changes made **after** the census snapshot. The table above keeps its original
line numbers and statuses; the entries below supersede them by symbol and group ID. No group is
promoted to `SAFETY_DISCHARGED`, and the section 4 register remains authoritative for retained
obligations.

| Group IDs | Change | Status after change | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `C001`; `C002`; `C583`; `C584`; `C676`; `C677` | `bits`, `bits_set`, `gsiv_flag`, `gsiv_set`, `cpp_flag`, `cpp_set` (and `ctx_enc`, which forwards to `bits`) are now `unsafe fn`s with a live/aligned/exclusive-access `# Safety` contract. Every call site already sits inside an unsafe block whose contract supplies the live context. | `CONTRACTED` | `cargo check --all-targets`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`; no safe caller can now dereference the pointer. A Miri NULL/dangling control is still not run, so this is not `ESTABLISHED`. |
| `C183`; `C184` | `aes_wrap_cipher_internal`'s `# Safety` now states the exact output extent (`up8(inlen)+8` encrypting, `inlen-8` decrypting), and its sole caller establishes it. The authority's `outsize < inl` check is retained first; a second check enforces the encrypting arm's actual extent (`docs/DECISIONS.md` D561), but only for a real write: NULL `input`, NULL `out` (the size-query arm) and input lengths the helper refuses before writing are skipped so the authority's result and error precedence are preserved. | `CONTRACTED` | `aes_wrap_cipher_refuses_a_padded_output_extent` (9-byte input, 16-byte destination, encryption + padding): returns 0 and leaves a `0xaa` canary untouched; `aes_wrap_cipher_length_query_is_not_preempted` (NULL `out`: 16/24-byte answers); `aes_wrap_cipher_null_input_returns_zero`; `aes_wrap_cipher_keeps_invalid_input_error_precedence` (reason 230, not 106). |
| `C187`; `C188` | The key-wrap row refuses a destination the authority's check admits (`outsize == inl` for encryption), but only for a write the authority would actually perform. Error site `PROV_CIPHER_AES_WRP_250` is reused, so the authority's defined refusals keep their source coordinate. | `CONTRACTED` | The four wrap regressions above; full lib suite. |
| `C516`; `C517` (and the SHA-256 twin) | `ptr::write_bytes` replaced by the crate's `OPENSSL_cleanse` (volatile writes + fence, `src/runtime/mem.rs`). The scratch buffer is now supplied to a body helper so the post-call bytes are observable. | `CONTRACTED` | New `set_mac_key_cleanses_its_scratch` observes a 64-byte scratch zeroed for both SHA-1 and SHA-256. No optimised stack-residue measurement, so not `ESTABLISHED`. |
| `C122`; `C123`; `C126`; `C132` (and the AES-XTS, SM4, SM4-XTS, ARIA, DES, OCB twins) | Whole-context `copy_nonoverlapping` replaced by `ptr::copy`, the defined equivalent of the authority's `*dctx = *sctx` including `dst == src`. The OCB hw `copyctx` stub is empty and unreachable -- `AES_OCB_FUNCTIONS` registers `aes_ocb_dupctx` for `OSSL_FUNC_CIPHER_DUPCTX`, and `ProvCipherHw::copyctx` is read only by the generic/XTS/SM4/ARIA duplicate bodies -- so its documentation now records that path and `aes_ocb_dupctx`'s own whole-context copy uses `ptr::copy`. | `CONTRACTED` | `copyctx_self_copy_is_defined` exercises self-copy for AES and TDES and asserts the repaired key-schedule pointer; the OCB dispatch row is quoted in the stub comment. |
| `C463`; `C464` | `aes_siv_dupctx` refuses a self-duplicate before acquiring references, and on `ossl_siv128_copy_ctx` failure releases the two cipher up-refs plus the partially built `cipher_ctx`/`mac_ctx_init`; `siv.mac` is never freed because `ossl_siv128_copy_ctx` never owns it on a failure return. | `CONTRACTED` | `siv_dupctx_refuses_a_self_duplicate` installs a real `EVP_CIPHER_CTX` sentinel and asserts it survives; `siv_dupctx_failure_releases_only_the_refs_it_acquired` drives the real `siv_dupctx` entry with an unarmed source `EVP_CIPHER_CTX` (so `ossl_siv128_copy_ctx` returns 0 after both up-refs) and asserts both dummy cipher refcounts return to 1, which rules out both a missed release and a double release; full lib suite. |

**R1 and R7 after the 2026-10-18 addendum.** `SyncCell`'s one-write-before-read discipline is now
enforced by `EXPORTED_CIPHERS_INIT: Once`, shared by the writer and every reader, and the `Sync`
impl is for the concrete array rather than generic `T` (see `PERFILE-AUDIT.md`). The eight legacy
`CipherDestroy` bodies were read against their authority `*_freectx` functions and executed on the
all-zero state by `every_legacy_cipher_destroy_body_is_zero_valid`; each is `reset_ctx`, whose
`alloced` guard makes the zero state a no-op. These are `ESTABLISHED` witnesses, not
`SAFETY_DISCHARGED`: `exported_ciphers()` still returns a raw pointer, and the generic `Drop` still
relies on the trait contract. No Miri/TSan evidence exists for the `Once` publication.

**Residual on the SIV failure arm.** The cleanup above depends on
`ossl_siv128_copy_ctx`'s failure invariant that `dest->mac` is either NULL or the source's unowned
pointer on every `0` return. If that function later acquires the MAC reference before a different
failure point, `aes_siv_dupctx` must be revisited or the MAC reference is leaked/double-freed.

**Gate evidence for this delta.** `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D
warnings`, and `cargo test --lib -- --test-threads=4` (1260 passed, 18 ignored, 0 failed) ran in the
court container; `rt_cipher_probe` (7291 observations) and `rt_cipher_mem_probe` (382 observations)
produced zero authority/candidate diffs. The phase8 probe binaries are the committed staged
instruments and were not rebuilt for this delta.
